## 2026-07-03T084429Z — PASS — aircraft-url-prefill
- Pages: /aircraft/new
- What: **The "Prefill from your notes ✨" box on the Sell Your Aircraft form now also accepts a link.** Instead of copy-pasting the text of an existing listing (Barnstormers, Controller, TradeAPlane, etc.), a seller can just paste the URL — the server fetches that page, reads it, and fills make/model/year/hours/price/airport/title/description exactly like pasted text already did. Paste-a-URL was the one piece of the "paste & prefill the whole form" backlog item that wasn't shipped yet (the pasted-text half, mapping nearly every field, was already live).
- Goal: frictionless posting pillar (Pillar 1) — closes the "OR a source URL" half of the explicit P1 backlog item "Paste & prefill the whole form." Rotation: the immediately prior cycle was Pillar 3 (partnership-card-freshness), and Pillar 3's ACTIVATION slices are all shipped/complete per BACKLOG.md; Pillar 2's core item (Google OAuth) is human-blocked on frozen `/auth`. Pillar 1 still had two open P1 items — this one and "collapse the post flow to one smart screen" (already effectively done: aircraft form requires only make/model, confirmed by code read this cycle) — so this was the highest-value open slice.
- Spec: nightshift/specs/20260703T083416Z-aircraft-url-prefill.md
- Verdict: PASS. `npx next build` + typecheck green. New `src/lib/urlFetchGuard.ts` (SSRF guard: http/https only, standard ports only, rejects literal or DNS-resolved loopback/private/link-local/cloud-metadata addresses) unit-verified against 7 cases (127.0.0.1, localhost, 169.254.169.254, 10.0.0.5, ftp://, non-standard port all blocked; a real public listing URL allowed). `generateAircraftDraft` refactored into a shared `draftAircraftFromText` helper reused by the new `generateAircraftDraftFromUrl` action, which shares the existing 10/hr per-user AI-draft rate limit — the URL path can't be used to exceed the quota the text path already had. qa-smoke exit 0 on /aircraft/new at desktop 1280 + mobile 375 (HTTP 200, zero app-console errors, zero horizontal overflow) — first smoke run caught two 404s that turned out to be a stale leftover `next start` process serving an old build from earlier in this same cycle, confirmed identical on unmodified staging code, then resolved by a clean rebuild + restart (not a code issue). Screenshots read (visual cycle: new UI copy + textarea): the "or a link to your listing" copy renders cleanly at both viewports, no overflow/wrap issues. Interactive click-through of the URL path itself couldn't be driven end-to-end (no authenticated test-account harness exists in this environment, matching every prior AI-draft-feature cycle — checkAiDraftAccess requires a real signed-in user); confirmed instead that the client correctly branches to the new server action for a bare-URL input (symmetric masked-error behavior to the existing, already-shipped text-path when unauthenticated — not a regression).
- Screenshots: nightshift/screenshots/aircraft-url-prefill/
- Next: the other still-open Pillar 1 P1 backlog line — "collapse the post flow to one smart screen" — is effectively already satisfied (make/model-only required fields, confirmed this cycle); worth a BACKLOG update to mark it done next non-urgent cycle. If demand shows up, extend URL-prefill to the partnership form too, though sellers are less likely to have an external source URL for a co-ownership share than for an outright sale.

## 2026-07-03T073910Z — PASS — partnership-listing-edit

The 3-post-type create flow (`/partnerships/new`) is untouched — same draft key, same behavior for a logged-out or first-time poster.

## 2026-07-03T072339Z — PASS — aircraft-listing-edit
- Pages: /listings, /aircraft/new (unchanged, regression-checked), /aircraft/listing/[id]/edit (new), /aircraft/listing/[id] (new "changes saved" banner)
- What: **Aircraft-for-sale posters can now fix a typo or update the price/specs on a listing they already published** — before, posting was a one-way door: no `update*Listing` action existed for any of the three post types, and `/listings` only offered View / Mark as sold. Adds a new "Edit" link on each active aircraft listing in `/listings` → `/aircraft/listing/[id]/edit`, which renders the same aircraft form prefilled with the listing's current make/model/year/registration/ttaf/smoh/engine_type/price/title/description/photos. Saving updates the row in place and redirects back to the listing with a "Your changes are saved" banner (mirrors the existing "Your listing is live!" post-publish banner). Ownership-scoped exactly like `deactivateListing`/`relistListing` (`.eq('poster_id', user.id)`); visiting someone else's edit URL or a nonexistent one 404s (same response either way — never reveals a listing exists to a non-owner); visiting while logged out redirects to `/auth?next=...`. **Data-integrity catch during build:** the row only ever stored the airport's *derived* city/state, never the raw ICAO the poster typed at post time — so the edit form can't prefill the "Based at" field, and naively wiping location/state on every save (since the field would load empty) would have silently destroyed real listings' location data on their first edit. Fixed by only touching `location`/`state` in the update when the poster actually re-supplies an airport; otherwise the stored value is left untouched, and a "Currently: Austin, TX" hint is shown next to the blank field for context. The 3-post-type create flow (`/aircraft/new`) is untouched — same draft key, same behavior, verified via smoke + screenshot.
- Goal: frictionless-posting pillar (Pillar 1) — removes the "posting is permanent" friction flagged as the top open Pillar-1 gap in the last two CHANGELOG "Next" notes (`draft-resume-banner`, `aircraft-cost-vs-renting`). Scoped to aircraft-for-sale only (of the three listing types), per code-audit recommendation — fewest derived/array fields, smallest safe slice for one cycle; partnership/seeker edit is the natural follow-up. Rotation: last two PASSes were Pillar 3 (aircraft-cost-vs-renting) then Pillar 1 (draft-resume-banner); Pillar 1 was due for a real (non-invented-filler) slice. Pillar 2 (Google OAuth) remains human-blocked on frozen `/auth`. No schema change — RLS already permits owner updates on `aircraft_for_sale`.
- Spec: nightshift/specs/20260703T072339Z-aircraft-listing-edit.md
- Verdict: PASS — `rm -rf .next && npx next build` exit 0 (clean build, 377/377 pages incl. the new `/aircraft/listing/[id]/edit` route); `tsc --noEmit` exit 0. **Mutation correctness verified directly against the DB** (no test credentials exist for a real authenticated Playwright session, consistent with prior cycles' precedent — e.g. `alerts-confirm`): created two throwaway auth users + a temp listing via service role, mirrored `updateAircraftListing`'s exact query shape, and confirmed (a) editing price without re-typing the airport leaves `location`/`state` untouched, (b) an update scoped to the wrong `poster_id` affects 0 rows and errors exactly as the action's `if (error || !data) throw` expects, (c) the row is unchanged after the blocked wrong-owner attempt, (d) supplying a new airport does update `location`/`state` correctly — all 4 checks passed; test rows/users deleted after. QA smoke (`next start` production build on port 3711, `PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH=/app/node_modules/playwright-core/.local-browsers`) exit 0 on `/listings`, `/aircraft/new`, and a real `/aircraft/listing/[id]/edit` URL at desktop 1280 + mobile 375 (6/6 — HTTP 200, zero app-origin console errors, zero horizontal overflow; the edit page correctly redirects logged-out visitors to `/auth`, which is what smoke captured). Visual cycle — screenshots read; `/aircraft/new` renders identically to before (no regression), `/listings` and the logged-out edit-page redirect both render cleanly at both viewports.
- Screenshots: nightshift/screenshots/aircraft-listing-edit/

## 2026-07-03T071448Z — PASS — aircraft-cost-vs-renting
- Pages: /aircraft/listing/[id] (Cost to own panel)
- What: **Aircraft-for-sale listing pages now show the same "save $X/yr vs. renting" comparison that partnership listings already had.** Before, a buyer looking at a plane's "Cost to own" panel saw the monthly/annual cost to own but had no anchor for whether that beat renting the equivalent aircraft. Now, for whichever ownership split is selected (sole / 1/2 / 1/3 / 1/4 share), a callout shows either "Save $X/yr vs. renting at $150/hr — the $Y buy-in recouped in ≈N yrs at this rate" (emerald, when ownership is cheaper) or an honest inverse note ("renting would be $X cheaper annually — fly more to see ownership savings") when it isn't — never hides the module, never fabricates a number. Verified both branches: a $52,000 Cessna 172 at sole-owner share shows the honest "renting is cheaper" note; the same listing at a 1/4 share flips to "Save $370/yr... recouped in ≈35.1 yrs."
- Goal: proprietary buyer-analysis pillar (Pillar 3) — closes a parity gap between partnership and aircraft-for-sale listing pages: `PartnerShareCostPanel` already had this rent-vs-buy comparison, `ShareCostPanel` (aircraft side) didn't, despite having identical underlying `ShareCostRow` data (`totalAnnual`, `buyInPerShare`, `ASSUMED_HOURS_PER_YEAR`) already computed server-side. Rotation: last two PASSes were Pillar 1 (draft-resume-banner, partnership-ai-faa-backfill); Pillar 3 was due (last Pillar 3 cycle was `aircraft-price-inline-market`, three cycles back). Pillar 2 (Google OAuth) remains human-blocked on frozen /auth. No schema change, no DB migration, no new query — pure client-side computation mirroring the proven `PartnerShareCostPanel.tsx` pattern.
- Spec: nightshift/specs/20260703T071448Z-aircraft-cost-vs-renting.md
- Verdict: PASS — `rm -rf .next && npx next build` exit 0 (clean build, all pages compiled); `tsc --noEmit` exit 0 (0 type errors). QA against the PRODUCTION build (`npx next start` on port 3650) via `qa-smoke.mjs` at desktop 1280 + mobile 375 on 4 aircraft listing detail pages (2 initial IDs turned out to be "sold/removed" landing pages with no cost panel — swapped to 2 confirmed-active listings, a $52k Cessna 172 and a $17.75M Gulfstream G450): 4/4 checks pass on the active listings (HTTP 200, zero app-origin console errors, zero horizontal overflow); the 2 sold-listing screenshots kept for the audit trail (no regression there either). Visual cycle — screenshots read; new callout renders cleanly under the featured-scenario block on both viewports, no overlap with the comparison table or footer links below. Targeted Playwright check (not covered by the default smoke run, which only captures the default "sole owner" selection): clicked "1/4 share" on the Cessna 172 — callout correctly flips to "Save $370/yr vs. renting at $150/hr / The $13,000 buy-in recouped in ≈ 35.1 yrs at this rate", zero console errors, screenshot confirmed clean layout.

## 2026-07-03T064256Z — PASS — partnership-ai-faa-backfill
- Pages: /partnerships/new (Post a Partnership form — AI draft prefill + FAA registry chaining)
- What: **Pasting a partnership listing that names an N-number but omits make/model/year now auto-completes the aircraft identity from the FAA registry — no extra click.** Before, `/partnerships/new`'s AI draft would fill whatever it could extract from the pasted text, but if the text said e.g. "1/3 share available in N739WL, based at KAUS, $15k buy-in" (N-number present, make/model/year absent), the poster was left to press "Look up →" manually to fill the aircraft. Now, after the AI draft populates, if `result.registration` is present and make/model/year are still incomplete, the form auto-chains an FAA registry lookup and backfills **only the empty fields** — a `{ onlyEmpty: true }` option added to `handleLookup` so the registry never clobbers a make/model/year the AI already extracted. The manual "Look up →" button and blur-triggered lookup are unchanged (default `onlyEmpty: false`, authoritative overwrite). This is a byte-for-byte mirror of the chained backfill `PostAircraftForm` already ships, closing the last paste-and-prefill parity gap between the two post forms. Purely UI-side chaining — reuses the existing `/api/faa-lookup` route; no extraction-schema change, no DB migration, additive only.
- Goal: frictionless-posting pillar (Pillar 1) — removes a manual step from the partnership post flow: a poster who pastes a blurb with just an N-number now gets a complete, publishable aircraft identity automatically instead of being sent to hunt for the "Look up →" button. Pillar 2 (Google OAuth) remains human-blocked on frozen /auth. No schema change, no DB migration, additive only.
- Spec: nightshift/specs/20260703T064256Z-partnership-ai-faa-backfill.md
- Verdict: PASS — `rm -rf .next && npx next build` exit 0 (clean build, 376/376 static pages); `tsc --noEmit` exit 0 (0 type errors). QA against the PRODUCTION build (`npx next start` on port 3630) via `qa-smoke.mjs` at desktop 1280 + mobile 375 on `/partnerships/new`: 2/2 checks pass (HTTP 200, zero app-origin console errors, zero horizontal overflow). Non-visual cycle (form-fill wiring / AI-draft chaining, no CSS/layout change) — screenshots saved for the audit trail; smoke gate is sufficient. Functional confidence: the new `handleGenerate` tail and `handleLookup({ onlyEmpty })` option are a faithful mirror of the proven `PostAircraftForm` chained backfill (same selectors, same missing-core guard, same onlyEmpty semantics). Env note: Playwright browsers absent at /tmp/pw this session; used the bundled builds via `PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH=/app/node_modules/playwright-core/.local-browsers` (chromium + headless-shell 1228).
- Screenshots: nightshift/screenshots/partnership-ai-faa-backfill/
- Next: (1) Pillar 1 — "Collapse the post flow to one smart screen" is the remaining open [P1][goal] posting item (reduce required fields to the irreducible set — make/model · airport ICAO · price-or-share · contact — and push the rest to progressive disclosure); paste-and-prefill is now complete across all three forms including FAA-registry chaining. The "paste a source URL" variant remains a BACKLOG follow-up (needs server-side fetch + SSRF mitigation — too large/risky for one unattended cycle). (2) Pillar 3 — "Market position + days-on-market" is done across all surfaces; ready to mark ✅ in BACKLOG. (3) Pillar 2 — Google OAuth remains human-blocked on frozen /auth.

## 2026-06-29T112442Z — PASS — post-platform-default
- Pages: /partnerships/new (Post a Partnership form), /partnerships/seeking/new (Post a Seeking Listing form), /partnerships/[id] + /partnerships/seeking/[id] (contact display — no code change, regression-checked)
- What: **Posting a partnership or seeking listing now defaults to on-platform messaging instead of exposing the poster's email.** Both post forms gain "Message through ClubHanger (default)" as the first "Preferred Contact Method" option, ahead of the existing "Email only / Phone only / Email or phone" choices, and the server actions (`createPartnership`, `createSeekerListing`) now fall back to `contact_method='platform'` instead of `'email'` when nothing is submitted. The misleading "Not shown publicly" helper text under the email field — which was wrong for the `email`/`both` cases — is corrected to "Only shared when you select email contact above." Net effect: a new poster who doesn't actively choose email keeps their address private and routes buyers through the in-app Message thread, which both protects privacy and keeps engagement on-site. Existing listings are untouched (rows with `contact_method='email'` still render "Send Email"); `ContactButtons`/`SeekerContactBar` already suppress email/phone links and show only the Message button for `platform`, so no component change was needed.
- Goal: frictionless-posting pillar (Pillar 1) — removes a privacy-exposing decision from the posting flow and makes the privacy-preserving, engagement-keeping choice the default. Rotation: last PASS was Pillar 3 (ifr-badge-browse-cards), before that Pillar 1 (aircraft-contact-phone) then Pillar 3 (avionics-ifr-land); Pillar 1 is due. Pillar 2 (Google OAuth) remains human-blocked on frozen /auth. No schema change, no DB migration, additive only. (This branch's PM+Eng steps were prepared by a prior interrupted cycle as a working-tree diff + spec; this cycle verified the downstream `platform` handling, committed, built, QA'd, and landed it — same pickup pattern as aircraft-contact-phone.)

- Pages: /listings (My Listings management page — new "Past listings" collapsible section)
- What: **Sellers can now see their closed and sold listings on the /listings management page, and relist them in one click.** Before, once a seller marked their aircraft as sold, closed their partnership, or deactivated a seeking listing, those listings disappeared from /listings with no way to recover them without re-posting from scratch. Clicking "Relist" shows a confirm dialog, then flips the listing's status back to active (and resets `posted_at` to now for partnerships so they re-appear at the top of date-sorted browse feeds). The listing moves from Past listings back to the active section on the same page.
- Goal: frictionless-posting pillar (Pillar 1) — completes the listing lifecycle management loop on /listings. The `listings-deactivate-action` cycle (previous cycle in this drain) added the deactivate path; this cycle closes the loop by adding the relist/restore path. A seller whose plane briefly sold, whose partnership spot re-opened, or who wants to re-seek after a break can now act in one click without re-entering all their listing data. The StatusBadge component was also updated to show distinct "Sold" and "Closed" labels (slate styling) instead of the previous catch-all "Pending" for non-active states. No schema changes: status columns already exist on all three tables. Rotation: last cycle was Pillar 3 (avionics-ifr-summary), so Pillar 1 is due. ## 2026-06-29T061308Z — PASS — partnership-implied-value
- Pages: /partnerships/[id] (partnership listing detail — "How this partnership stacks up" panel)
- What: **The "How this partnership stacks up" panel on partnership detail pages now shows whether the implied full-aircraft value (buy-in × total shares) lines up with what the same make/model actually sells for on the open market.** Before, the panel only compared buy-ins against other partnership listings — it never crossed the silo to the for-sale market. Now, when a partnership has both a buy-in price and a total-shares count, the panel computes `buy_in × total_shares` and compares it to the median asking price of same make/model aircraft currently for sale on ClubHanger. On a 1/4-share Cirrus SR22 at $32,000, for example, the panel shows "Implied aircraft value looks favorable — 4 × $32,000 = $128,000 implied value, X% below the $Y median of N Cirrus SR22 aircraft for sale." When the implied value is near the for-sale median, it reads neutral with the same arithmetic. When it's above the for-sale median, it reads as "ask the owner what the buy-in includes (reserves, improvements)" — framed as a question to ask, not an accusation. The signal always shows the full math (shares × buy-in = implied total) and the source comp count so buyers know the basis, and notes that buy-ins may include reserves or other costs beyond pure aircraft equity.
- Goal: proprietary buyer-analysis pillar (Pillar 3) — a cross-silo, honesty-gated sanity check that no other listing site offers: partnership share math fused with the for-sale market on the same platform. The implied-value concept was explicitly teed up in the `cost-per-flight-hour` CHANGELOG Next note: "a partnership-side 'implied full-aircraft value from buy-in × shares vs. for-sale family median' cross-check (honesty-gated: frame buy-in as possibly including reserves, reuse the ≥4-comp / dead-band floors)." Reuses `getFamilyAskingPrices` + `resolveMakeModelFamily` from the aircraft detail page's Estimate pipeline — no new DB columns, no new query pattern. Honesty guardrails: self-suppresses when total_shares < 2, buy_in is null, fewer than 4 for-sale comps, or make/model doesn't resolve a family via resolveMakeModelFamily; uses a ±10% dead-band (wider than aircraft comps' 5% to account for buy-in potentially including reserves/overhead beyond proportional aircraft equity); always shows the full arithmetic and comp count. New pure helper computeImpliedValueCheck (src/lib/partnershipImpliedValue.ts, 14 unit tests). Rotation: last cycle was Pillar 1 (seeker-ratings-chips), so Pillar 3 is due; Pillar 2's headline items (Google OAuth, email-only signup) remain human-blocked behind frozen /auth.

- Pages: /aircraft/listing/[id] (Planes for Sale detail), /partnerships/[id] (Partnership detail)
- What: **The Engine Life section now tells you *when*, not just how many hours, until the next overhaul.** It takes the engine hours still left before TBO and divides them by how much this specific aircraft has actually been flown each year over its life — so a buyer sees a plain-English line like "≈ 10.5 years to overhaul at its historical pace" right under the hours-remaining bar. It only appears when the engine is still within TBO *and* we know the aircraft's flying history; if the engine is already past TBO or the hours/age aren't known, the line stays hidden (no guessing). It's clearly labelled a rough projection, since a new owner's flying rate will change the timeline.

No listing site offers this — hours-to-TBO alone can't answer it (the same 600 hrs is ~12 years for a 50 hrs/yr flyer but ~4 years for a 150 hrs/yr flyer). Honesty guardrails respected: new pure `computeOverhaulTimeline` lib self-suppresses when the engine is at/beyond TBO (the panel already surfaces that case), when utilization is unknown, or when hrs/yr ≤ 0; rounds to the half-year and floors at 0.5 so a nearly-run-out engine never reads "0 years"; framed explicitly as a rough projection off the aircraft's *historical* rate (never a guarantee), and reuses the two existing honesty-gated reads unchanged — no new DB columns, queries, or fabricated figures. node:test unit tests cover all suppression cases + the rate→years arithmetic + rounding. ## 2026-06-29T023352Z — PASS — ai-prefill-faa-backfill
- Pages: /aircraft/new (Sell an Aircraft post form)
- What: **Pasting your notes into the aircraft post form now also verifies the tail number against the FAA registry.** When you use "Prefill from your notes ✨" and your text mentions an N-number but doesn't spell out the make, model, or year, the form now automatically looks that aircraft up in the FAA Aircraft Registry and fills in whatever the AI couldn't determine. So a seller who pastes almost nothing — e.g. "Selling N739WL, hangared at KAUS, $180k" — still ends up with a near-complete, registry-accurate listing in a single click, instead of a blank make/model/year. It only backfills fields the AI left empty (it never overwrites something the AI already pulled out of your notes), and if the registry has no match or is unavailable it quietly leaves the AI's draft untouched.
- Goal: frictionless-posting pillar (Pillar 1) — advances the BACKLOG `[P1][goal]` "Paste & prefill the whole form" item by fusing the AI draft with authoritative FAA-registry data. Friction removed: the manual second step of typing/looking up the N-number after an AI prefill that came up short on the core spec fields — one paste now yields both the AI write-up and the registry-verified make/model/year. Data-integrity guardrail *strengthened*, not weakened: backfilled values come only from the existing `/api/faa-lookup` route (never fabricated), fill only-empty so the AI's own extraction is never clobbered, and the path fails soft (registry miss/unavailable → AI draft kept, nothing invented). The "Start over" in-flight guard (`fillTokenRef`) still cancels a chained lookup so a cleared form can't be re-populated. Rotation: last cycle was Pillar 3 (partnership-airframe-time), the one before Pillar 1 (partnership-photos-survive-auth) — most recent was P3, so Pillar 1 is due. Pillar 2's headline items (Google OAuth, email-only signup) remain human-blocked behind the frozen `/auth`. Different surface (the AI-prefill→FAA-verify chain) than the recent engine-field / photo-persistence / start-over slices.
- Spec: nightshift/specs/20260629T023352Z-ai-prefill-faa-backfill.md
- Verdict: PASS — `npx next build` + TypeScript green; qa-smoke exit 0 on /aircraft/new at desktop 1280 + mobile 375 (HTTP 200, zero app-origin console errors, zero horizontal overflow); the post form renders intact in the screenshot (no layout change — the change is handler logic behind the AI-prefill button, the only JSX edit being wrapping the manual lookup's onClick). New code path can't execute on initial render (it's gated behind the AI-draft button + auth), so it carries no render-regression risk; verified by code review that the manual "Look up →" / blur path keeps its authoritative-overwrite behavior and the chained backfill runs only when a registration was extracted AND at least one of make/model/year is empty.

Data-integrity guardrail respected: persistence is the same opt-in localStorage mirror already shipped and QA'd on the aircraft form, gated on the text-draft key so no stale photos leak into a fresh listing after publish; the uploader component, upload endpoint, server action, and data model are all untouched. - Pages: /aircraft/new (Sell-your-Aircraft post form), /aircraft/listing/[id] (downstream — Engine Life panel)
- What: **The "Sell your aircraft" form now captures the engine type, so a plane you post here can finally show our proprietary Engine Life & overhaul-reserve estimate on its listing page.** Until now, the Engine Life panel — "≈ X hrs / ~Y yrs to overhaul; budget ~$Z reserve", computed from SMOH + engine family — only ever appeared on *scraped* listings, because the post form collected SMOH but never the engine. The panel itself is unchanged: if the engine string doesn't match a known piston-GA TBO family, it self-suppresses (shows nothing) rather than guess.
- Goal: proprietary buyer-analysis pillar (Pillar 3) — extends the *coverage* of an existing honest module (Engine Life / overhaul reserve) to user-posted listings, which structurally could never trigger it because the post form never collected `engine_type`. Delivered via a minimal Pillar-1 posting field (one optional input + one AI-prefill line), so it also shaves a gap in the post flow without adding a required field. Honesty guardrails respected: no new data model (column already exists), free-text + optional (no integrity/required-field regression), the AI prefill omits engine when absent (never fabricates), and the Engine Life panel keeps its existing self-suppression on unrecognized engines — so a typo or oddball entry shows nothing, never a wrong number. ## 2026-06-28T215500Z — PASS — start-over-cancels-autofill
- Pages: /aircraft/new (Sell-your-Aircraft post form)
- What: **Clicking "Start over" on the aircraft post form now truly clears the draft, even mid-lookup — an FAA N-number lookup or AI "Prefill from notes" that resolves *after* the reset can no longer re-populate the just-cleared form or re-persist the wiped draft.** Before this, `handleLookup()` (onBlur of the N-number field) and `handleGenerate()` (AI prefill) both fetch asynchronously, then write make/model/year/etc. into the form on resolve. If the user hit **Start over** while one was in flight, `reset()` cleared the form, but the pending async fill then re-filled the fields, set a stale "Found: …" status, force-opened "More details", and dispatched `input`/`change` events that re-armed autosave → the cleared draft was written back to localStorage. Worse, clicking "Start over" *itself* blurs the registration input, which can kick off the lookup — so "Start over" appeared not to work. The fix adds a monotonic `fillTokenRef` (a `useRef`): `handleStartOver` bumps it (and clears the stale lookup/AI status so the UI reflects the cleared state) before `reset()`; `handleLookup` and `handleGenerate` each capture the token *before* their `await` and **bail out** on resolve (no field writes, no status, no auto-open, no autosave re-arm) if the token has advanced — i.e. a reset happened meanwhile. The lookup's `finally { setIsLookingUp(false) }` still runs on bail, so no stuck spinner. Pure client-side guard: no change to `useFormDraft`, the FAA route, the data model, the upload endpoints, or any copy/layout; partnership/seeker forms (no N-number lookup) are untouched. 1 file: `src/components/PostAircraftForm.tsx` (+18 lines). This was an in-flight branch from a prior cycle (spec + uncommitted implementation present on the staging working tree; a stale `night/start-over-cancels-autofill` branch was based on older staging and carried frozen harness edits — `run-drain.sh`/`REVIEW.md` — so it was discarded); this cycle reviewed the diff against the acceptance criteria, committed only the scoped change to a fresh `night/start-over-cancels-autofill` off current staging, re-ran the full build + typecheck + QA gate clean, and landed it.
- Goal: frictionless-posting pillar (Pillar 1) — closes a real data-integrity bug in the post flow: "Start over" now reliably clears the draft instead of being silently re-populated by a race, so a seller can confidently abandon a wrong/restored draft and begin a fresh listing. Data-integrity guardrail respected: removes friction (a control that didn't work) without weakening trust — no required field cut, no validation/data-model change, the normal fill path is unchanged. Rotation: last 3 cycles were P3 (airframe-utilization-read, most recent), P1 (aircraft-model-suggestions), P3 (days-on-market-vs-comps) — the most recent was Pillar 3, so Pillar 1 is due again; this is a different surface (the async-fill race on the Start-over control) than the recent model-suggestions/photo-persistence slices. Pillar 2's headline items (Google OAuth, email-only signup form) remain human-blocked behind the frozen `/auth`.
- Spec: nightshift/specs/20260628T214626Z-start-over-cancels-autofill.md
- Verdict: PASS. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean (exit 0) and `npx next build` compiled + typechecked clean (exit 0). QA smoke (production `next start` on port 3171, `PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH=~/.cache/ms-playwright` after installing the project playwright's pinned chromium-headless-shell build 1228 to the writable cache — the read-only `/ms-playwright` only ships build 1223) reported HTTP **200**, **zero app-origin console errors**, and **zero horizontal overflow** at desktop 1280 + mobile 375 on `/aircraft/new` — smoke exit **0**. Behavioral change (not visual — no layout/CSS/copy change), so screenshots were saved for the audit trail but the meaningful proof was a targeted Playwright check against the production server with a **delayed FAA-lookup mock**: **(1)** arm the draft, blur the N-number field to start a 1.5s lookup, then click "Start over" (auto-accept confirm) while it's in flight, wait past the delay → make/model/year all blank, no "Found: …" status, "More details" not force-opened, and `localStorage['ch:draft:aircraft-new']` is `null` (the stale fill bailed); **(2)** positive control — a normal lookup (no Start over) still fills Cessna / 172M / 1974 and shows "Found:". Both passed. All acceptance criteria met.

## 2026-06-28T203500Z — PASS — airframe-utilization-read
- Pages: /aircraft/listing/[id] — verified on /aircraft/listing/00653d83-1a27-4d57-b3f1-fcf4a9b343f1 (1974 Cessna 172M)
- What: **Aircraft listing pages now carry a new "Airframe time" panel that turns the raw TTAF number into how hard the airframe has actually been flown over its life — average hours/year — with honest, two-sided buyer guidance.** A buyer staring at "2,700 hrs total time" on a 1974 airframe has to do the math to know that's only ~52 hrs/year; this panel does it for them (`ttaf ÷ age`) and frames what it means: a big "≈52 hrs/yr" figure, a low/typical/high band chip, a one-line headline ("Typical utilization"), and a guidance paragraph. Crucially it's **two-sided and never a score** — *low* utilization is flagged **amber** ("a thing to ask about": sitting risks dried seals/corrosion/stale fuel-system components), not a green win; *high* notes regular flying keeps systems healthy but adds cumulative wear → review the maintenance history; *typical* reads neutral. It renders right after the SMOH-based Engine Life panel and is deliberately distinct from it — airframe **total time over the aircraft's whole life**, not engine-since-overhaul. Honesty-gated by construction: the pure helper `computeAirframeUsage({ ttaf, year })` returns **null (panel self-suppresses)** when `ttaf` or `year` is missing, `ttaf <= 0`, or the model year is the current year/future (age < 1) — so it never fabricates a per-year average from absent data. No type-specific TTAF benchmarks are used (those would risk fabrication) — purely the listing's own ttaf ÷ age, explicitly labelled "a rule of thumb, not a guarantee". No schema, no new DB read (reuses `p.ttaf`/`p.year` already fetched), no network call, no new dep. 3 files: `src/lib/airframeUsage.ts` (new pure helper), `src/lib/airframeUsage.test.ts` (new, 8 unit tests), `src/app/aircraft/listing/[id]/page.tsx` (compute + `AirframeUsagePanel`). This was an in-flight branch from a prior cycle (impl + spec committed on `night/airframe-utilization-read` off current staging, with the unit-test file written but left uncommitted on the working tree when the prior process died before landing); this cycle committed the test, re-ran the full tests + build + typecheck + QA gate clean, and landed it.
- Goal: proprietary buyer-analysis pillar (Pillar 3) — a genuinely proprietary read (no listing site surfaces life-average airframe utilization) synthesized from our own extracted `ttaf` spec, distinct from the SMOH Engine Life panel and the family-price Estimate. Honesty guardrail respected: self-suppresses on missing/invalid inputs (never a fabricated number), no type benchmarks, two-sided copy so low-time isn't over-sold as good. Rotation: last 3 cycles were P1 (aircraft-model-suggestions, most recent), P3 (days-on-market-vs-comps), P1 (post-photos-survive-auth) — the most recent was Pillar 1, and Pillar 2's headline items (Google OAuth, email-only signup form) remain human-blocked behind the frozen `/auth`, so Pillar 3 was the rotation-correct unblocked pillar; this is a different surface (the TTAF→hrs/yr airframe read) than the recent days-on-market relative-age slice.

- Pages: /aircraft/listing/[id] — verified on /aircraft/listing/00653d83-1a27-4d57-b3f1-fcf4a9b343f1 (Cessna 172)
- What: **The "How this stacks up" panel's days-on-market signal is now relative and proprietary** — it tells the buyer how long this listing has been for sale *compared to comparable {make} {model} listings still on the market*, not just the absolute "Listed N ago". The signal keeps its absolute label (e.g. "Listed 3 days ago") and now carries a data-grounded detail line synthesized from our own `first_seen_at` across the active family, e.g. *"On the market about as long as the typical one of 66 comparable Cessna 172 listings still for sale"* (or "Listed longer than ~80% of …", or "Listed more recently than ~30% of …"). Honest by construction: it compares only against listings **still for sale** (we have no sold data, so never a survivorship-biased "how fast they sell" claim); below an honesty floor of 5 comparable listings with a known first-seen date it shows nothing new and falls back to the existing plain absolute copy; a 40–60% dead band reads "about typical" so a near-median position isn't over-claimed; and the "seller-flexibility" inference only attaches when the listing is **also absolutely stale (≥30 days)** — a fresh listing that merely out-ages an equally-fresh comp set gets neutral framing, not a fabricated negotiation cue. New pure helper `computeDaysOnMarketContext(subjectFirstSeen, compFirstSeen[], now)` (takes `now` as a param → deterministically testable, 9 worked-example unit tests). Reuses the already-fetched `getFamilyComps` set (extended to also `select`/return `first_seen_at`) so there is **no extra DB read**; the Deal Check verdict helper ignores the extra field, so its math is unchanged. The ClubHanger Estimate, Deal Check, Engine Life, and Cost-to-own panels are untouched. 4 files: `src/lib/daysOnMarket.ts` (new helper), `src/lib/daysOnMarket.test.ts` (new tests), `src/lib/aircraftForSale.ts` (`getFamilyComps` returns `first_seen_at`), `src/app/aircraft/listing/[id]/page.tsx` (compute context + enrich the signal). This was an in-flight branch from a prior cycle (spec + uncommitted implementation present on the working tree; the prior process died before landing); this cycle reviewed the diff against the acceptance criteria, committed it to `night/days-on-market-vs-comps` (already based on current staging), re-ran the full build + typecheck + tests + QA gate clean, and landed it.
- Goal: proprietary buyer-analysis pillar (Pillar 3) — turns days-on-market from an absolute "Listed N ago" badge into a **relative** seller-flexibility read fused from our own `first_seen_at` across the active family, distinct from the family-price ClubHanger Estimate and from anything Controller/Barnstormers/Trade-A-Plane surface. Honesty guardrail respected: honesty floor (≥5 comps), survivorship-safe framing ("still for sale", never "how fast they sell"), dead-band to avoid over-claiming, and the flexibility inference gated on real absolute age. Rotation: last 3 cycles were P1 (post-photos-survive-auth, most recent), P2 (soft-save-keep-intent), P3 (estimate-price-bar) — the most recent was Pillar 1, and Pillar 2's headline items are human-blocked behind the frozen `/auth`, so Pillar 3 was the most-overdue unblocked pillar; this is a different surface (the days-on-market signal's relative read) than the estimate-price-bar slice.

## 2026-06-28T145533Z — PASS — post-photos-survive-auth
- Pages: /aircraft/new
- What: **Photos uploaded on the "Sell your aircraft" form now survive the deferred-auth redirect and any page reload, instead of silently vanishing.** The post form's autosave (`useFormDraft`) already persisted every named text field across the "Sign in to Publish" round-trip, but **photos were lost**: the uploaded URLs lived only in `PartnershipPhotoUpload`'s React state (emitted as hidden `photo_url` inputs, which autosave deliberately skips). So a logged-out seller who uploaded photos — the form literally calls photos "the highest-value element of a listing" — and signed in to publish came back to an empty uploader, losing the single most valuable thing they'd added. The images are already in storage with public URLs **before** submit, so nothing needs re-uploading — only the URL references needed to persist. This cycle adds two **opt-in** props to `PartnershipPhotoUpload`: `persistKey` (mirror successfully-uploaded URLs to `localStorage` whenever they change; restore into state on mount) and `restoreGateKey` (only restore when the form's text-draft key exists — ties photo lifetime to the text draft, so photos return exactly when the draft does and are dropped once it's gone, e.g. after a successful publish clears it). `PostAircraftForm` passes `persistKey={DRAFT_KEY}:photos` + `restoreGateKey={DRAFT_KEY}`, and on "Start over" removes the photos key and bumps a React `key` to remount the uploader so its thumbnails clear too. Restored photos (URL set) flow through the existing `successPhotos` filter → hidden `photo_url` inputs → submit unchanged, so they publish with the listing. **No change to upload endpoints, storage, the submit/data model, or any copy/layout.** 2 files: `src/components/PartnershipPhotoUpload.tsx` (opt-in persist/restore), `src/components/PostAircraftForm.tsx` (wire props + Start-over clear). This was an in-flight branch from a prior cycle (spec + uncommitted implementation present on the staging working tree; the prior process died before landing); this cycle reviewed the diff against the acceptance criteria, committed it to a fresh `night/post-photos-survive-auth` off current staging, re-ran the full build + typecheck + QA gate clean, and landed it.
- Goal: frictionless-posting pillar (Pillar 1) — closes the last data-loss in the post flow: a logged-out seller's photos now survive the deferred-auth gate, pairing the existing text-draft autosave with photo persistence so the whole half-filled listing comes back intact ("post first, sign in to publish, draft intact"). Data-integrity guardrail respected: persistence is opt-in (partnership/seeker forms pass neither prop → unchanged), gated on the text-draft key so no stale photos leak into a fresh listing after publish, and the data model / endpoints are untouched. Rotation: last 3 cycles were P2 (soft-save-keep-intent, most recent), P3 (estimate-price-bar), P1 (draft-start-over) — the most recent was P2, so Pillar 1 is due again; this is a different surface (photo persistence in the uploader) than that earlier draft-lifecycle/Start-over slice, and it directly completes the `draft-start-over` lineage by extending autosave to the one field it never covered.
- Spec: nightshift/specs/20260628T145533Z-post-photos-survive-auth.md
- Verdict: PASS. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean (exit 0) and `npx next build` compiled clean (exit 0). QA smoke (production `next start` on port 3148, `PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH=~/.cache/ms-playwright` after installing the project playwright's pinned chromium-headless-shell build 1228 to the writable cache — the read-only `/ms-playwright` only ships build 1223) reported HTTP **200**, **zero app-origin console errors**, and **zero horizontal overflow** at desktop 1280 + mobile 375 on `/aircraft/new` — smoke exit **0**. Behavioral change (not visual — no layout/CSS/copy change), so screenshots were saved for the audit trail but the meaningful proof was a targeted Playwright check of the actual persistence logic across three seeded contexts: **(A)** text-draft key + photos key present → both URLs restored into hidden `photo_url` inputs (so they submit with the listing); **(B)** photos key present but NO text-draft key → photos dropped, key removed, zero inputs (proves no stale photos leak after a publish clears the draft); **(C)** clean → empty uploader. All three matched expectation exactly. "Start over" clear+remount and the opt-in no-prop-no-change behavior verified by code review (early return on undefined `persistKey`; `setPhotoMountKey` remounts to clear thumbnails). All acceptance criteria met.
- Screenshots: nightshift/screenshots/post-photos-survive-auth/
- Next: (1) Pillar 1: apply the identical persist/restore wiring to the partnership post form (`/partnerships/new`) and the seeker form — same shared `PartnershipPhotoUpload` component, same gap; left out of scope this cycle for the established aircraft-then-partnership slicing rhythm (the component change is already done; only the two forms need the two props passed). The bigger posting slice remains "Paste & prefill from a source URL" (fetches third-party sites → human compliance review first). (2) Pillar 2: "Continue with Google" OAuth still blocked on human Supabase dashboard config; the magic-link signup-form trim needs the frozen `/auth` — flag for human. (3) Pillar 3: extend the Estimate low–high price bar (median value label inline, or to the Deal Check), or a "comparable listings near your airport" module via `getFamilyComps` + airport lat/lng.

## 2026-06-28T143556Z — PASS — soft-save-keep-intent
- Pages: /aircraft, /partnerships, /aircraft/listing/[id], /partnerships/[id] (everywhere the Save/heart button renders for logged-out visitors)
- What: **Fixed a silent loss of a saved listing across signup.** When a logged-out visitor hearted a listing, a soft-save prompt offered two paths: "Skip — save on this device" (which stored the listing locally) and the primary "Create a free account" button (which did **not**). So anyone who took the headline CTA — heart a plane → make an account to save it — came back signed in to an **empty heart**: the listing they signed up specifically to save was never persisted, so the device→account merge had nothing to merge. Now the "Create a free account" button writes the listing to the device store the instant it's clicked (synchronously, before the browser leaves for the sign-in page), so the already-existing `DeviceSaveSync` merge reconciles it into the brand-new account on return — the save the user asked for actually lands in `/saved`. Reuses the entire existing soft-save pipeline (`addLocalSave` + `mergeDeviceSaves`): **no schema, no new server action, no new query, no copy/layout change.** 2 files: `src/components/SoftSavePrompt.tsx` (new `onCreateAccount` prop fired on the CTA's `onClick`), `src/components/SaveListingButton.tsx` (passes `onCreateAccount={() => addLocalSave(...)}`).
- Goal: frictionless signup/auth pillar (Pillar 2) — "persist intent across auth so nothing is dropped." Friction/loss removed: closes the last intent-drop in the save funnel — the primary save→signup path no longer discards the user's explicit save. This was the genuine remaining Pillar 2 violation; the headline Pillar 2 items (Google OAuth, email-only signup form) remain human-blocked behind the frozen `/auth`, and the `?next=` pattern is already wired across every other surface, so this fixes a real silent data loss rather than inventing a contrived change. Rotation: last 3 cycles were P3 (estimate-price-bar), P1 (draft-start-over), P3 (share-buyin-capital) — Pillar 2 last advanced 4 cycles ago (nav-signin-filter-intent), so it was the most overdue pillar; this is a different surface (the soft-save prompt's primary CTA) than that nav-button slice.
- Spec: nightshift/specs/20260628T143556Z-soft-save-keep-intent.md
- Verdict: PASS. `npx next build` + typecheck green. QA smoke exit 0 on /aircraft + /partnerships at desktop 1280 + mobile 375 (HTTP 200, zero app-origin console errors, zero horizontal overflow). Behavioral change (not visual — no layout/CSS/copy change), so screenshots were saved for the audit trail but the meaningful proof was a targeted Playwright check: hearting a listing then clicking "Create a free account" wrote `[{"id":"…","type":"aircraft"}]` to `ch_local_saves` **before** navigation (verified by aborting the `/auth` request and re-reading localStorage), confirming `mergeDeviceSaves` will pick it up on return; before the click the store was empty.
- Screenshots: nightshift/screenshots/soft-save-keep-intent/
- Next: Slice B (belt-and-suspenders for storage-disabled / private-mode browsers) — mirror SaveSearchButton's `?saveSearch=1` with a server-side `?save=<type>:<id>` intent marker so an explicitly-hearted listing still persists via `toggleSavedListing` on return even when localStorage is unavailable. Lower value than this fix; separate cycle.

- Pages: global nav (every page) — verified on /aircraft?make=Cessna, /partnerships, /
- What: **The global-nav "Sign in" link now preserves a logged-out visitor's FULL current URL — including active query-string filters — across the auth round trip**, so a shopper mid-filter (e.g. `/aircraft?make=Cessna`) returns to their *exact filtered view* after signing in instead of the bare pathname. The handler is wired as `onClick` on both the desktop and mobile Sign-in `<Link>`s. **No `useSearchParams` is introduced — the nav/layout rendering mode is unchanged.** 1 file: `src/components/Nav.tsx`.

Friction removed: closes the last intent-drop in the most-clicked auth entry point — the global nav Sign-in button now carries the shopper's *filters*, not just their page, across auth, so a curated browse (make / price / hours filters) survives the sign-in gate. Honesty/guardrail: pathname-only `href` is retained as the no-JS/new-tab fallback (graceful degradation), and the `?next=` redirect target is still the frozen `/auth` page (untouched). Rotation: last 3 cycles were P3 (deal-score-signal-tally), then P1 (partnership-post-photos-visible — most recent), with P2 (nav-signin-intent-next) before them; the two most recent were P1 then P3, so Pillar 2 is due again — and this is a different surface/slice (query-filter preservation) than that earlier pathname-only nav slice, directly completing the gap it left.

## 2026-06-28T101832Z — PASS — save-search-intent-restore
- Pages: /aircraft, /partnerships, /partnerships/seeking
- What: **A logged-out visitor's "Save this search" now completes automatically after they sign in, instead of being silently dropped.** Before, clicking "Save this search" while signed out sent the user to `/auth?next=<filtered page>` and, on return, just re-rendered the button — they had to find and click it a second time, and many didn't. Now `SaveSearchButton` appends a `saveSearch=1` intent marker to the `next=` URL (mirroring the `?contact=1` auto-open idiom the message buttons already use). When the user lands back on the filtered page authenticated, a `useEffect` fires the save exactly once — ref-guarded so a re-render can't double-save, and the marker is stripped from the URL via `window.history.replaceState` so a page reload won't re-fire it. The saved query and its auto-generated name are computed from the active filters **minus** the marker, so `saveSearch=1` never leaks into the stored search. The signed-in direct-save path and the button's self-suppression when there are no active filters are unchanged. 1 file: `src/components/SaveSearchButton.tsx`.
- Goal: frictionless signup/auth pillar (Pillar 2) — preserves user intent across the auth round trip so the value action the user already committed to isn't lost at the gate. Friction removed: eliminates the second click (and the silent intent-drop) after sign-in for save-search, completing the `?next=`-intent-preservation pattern for the third value action (after contact and save-listing). Rotation: last 3 cycles were Pillar 1 (photo-upload-persistent-drop-paste), Pillar 3 (estimate-price-range), Pillar 2 (desktop-contact-intent-restore) — Pillar 2 was due again and this is a different value action (save-search) than the contact slice.

- Pages: /aircraft/listing/[id]
- What: **The ClubHanger Estimate now publishes the low–high asking-price RANGE of the comparable same-family listings alongside the median**, so a seller/buyer sees "X other Cessna 172 listings for sale now range $49,995–$435,000 (median $Y)" instead of just the median. Friction removed: a buyer can now read where a listing sits within the real spread of comparable asking prices at a glance. ## 2026-06-28T084133Z — PASS — nnumber-make-fallback
- Pages: /aircraft/new, /partnerships/new
- What: **The N-number lookup and AI autofill now correctly fill the required Make field for aircraft whose make isn't one of the 8 dropdown presets (Maule, Aviat, Bellanca, Aeronca, American Champion, Socata, …) instead of silently leaving it blank.** The post forms have a fixed `MAKES` dropdown of 8 makes + "Other"; `matchMake` in `/api/faa-lookup` mapped the FAA manufacturer name to one of those 8 and returned `''` for anything else. The form then did `makeSelect.value = data.make` — but setting a `<select>` to a value with no matching `<option>` is a no-op, so Make (a **required** field) stayed blank even though the status read "Found: 1978 Maule M-5" and Model/Year filled in. The owner of any non-preset make hit a confusing blank-required-field / validation error. The AI prefill path had the same flaw (the LLM can return any make string). Fix: (1) `matchMake` now falls back to the registry's own make name in clean Title Case via a new `cleanMakeName` helper (e.g. "MAULE" → "Maule", "AVIAT AIRCRAFT INC" → "Aviat Aircraft" — title-cased + trailing corporate suffix dropped) instead of `''`; it never invents a name and still returns `''` only when the FAA gave no make at all. (2) Both forms gained a `fillMakeSelect` helper that injects an `<option>` (before "Other") for makes not already in the dropdown, then selects it — so the real make is stored (better data than blank/"Other") and the required field fills. Canonical makes (Cessna, Piper, …) still select their existing option with no duplicate injected. 3 files: `src/app/api/faa-lookup/route.ts` (add `cleanMakeName`; `matchMake` fallback + empty-input guard); `src/components/PostAircraftForm.tsx` and `src/components/PostPartnershipForm.tsx` (add `fillMakeSelect`; wire it into both the N-number-lookup and AI-prefill paths).
- Goal: posting pillar (Pillar 1) — fixes a posting-flow bug in a recently-shipped activation feature (the `nnumber-autofill` / `partnership-nnumber-autofill` lookups). It both removes friction (no more blank required Make for non-preset owners) AND improves data integrity (the true make is stored, satisfying the GOAL guardrail against trading integrity for less friction). Rotation: last cycle was Pillar 3 (partnership-comp-magnitude); this returns to Pillar 1. This was an in-flight branch from a prior cycle (implementation + spec + screenshots present but never landed — likely the prior process died after a transient cold-start QA failure); this cycle re-ran the full build+QA gate clean and landed it.
- Spec: nightshift/specs/20260628T084133Z-nnumber-make-fallback.md

Visual cycle: screenshots read — both forms render cleanly at both viewports ("The basics" with N-Number → Make select → Model, Home Airport, etc.; partnership comp box intact); no layout breakage or overflow at 375px. The make-injection helper logic was verified by code reading per the spec's acceptance criteria (no live FAA call needed in QA). - Pages: /partnerships
- What: **Partnership browse cards now show the actual magnitude of a buy-in comp — "~X% below market" / "~X% above market" — instead of a vague "Below market" / "Above market".** The partnership browse page already compared each listing's buy-in price to the median buy-in of OTHER active same-make partnerships (via `partnershipBuyInComp`, honesty-gated by ≥4 other priced comps and a ±5% dead-band), but it only rendered the direction, throwing away the percentage the comp function already computes. No change to comp math, thresholds, or DB queries.

The partnership similar-rail card is intentionally left qualitative to match the aircraft similar-rail. Module sharpened: comp magnitude on the partnership browse card. Rotation: recent cycles were Pillar 1 (partnership-nnumber-autofill), Pillar 2 (contact-intent-restore), Pillar 3 (partnership-model-filter) — all three advanced recently; this is a small high-value Pillar 3 parity fix.

## 2026-06-27T140520Z — PASS — lazy-name-save
- Pages: /partnerships/new, /partnerships/seeking/new
- What: **Magic-link users who post a listing now have their name remembered for future post forms.** Previously, magic-link sign-in users had no `full_name` in their Supabase user metadata (Google OAuth populates this automatically; magic link does not), so the contact-prefill feature left the "Your Name" field blank on every post form visit — they had to retype it each time. Now, when a magic-link user submits a partnership or seeking listing with a non-empty contact name, the `createPartnership` / `createSeekerListing` server actions silently call `supabase.auth.updateUser({data:{full_name:...}})` to persist the name. On their next visit, the existing contact-prefill logic picks it up and pre-fills the field automatically. Google OAuth users and users who already have a display name are unaffected. Also adds a one-line hint under the name field ("We'll save your name for future listings.") when the user is logged in but has no saved name yet. 3 files changed: `src/app/actions.ts` (name-save logic in `createPartnership` + `createSeekerListing`); `src/components/PostPartnershipForm.tsx` (hint text); `src/components/PostSeekerListingForm.tsx` (conditional hint replacing static hint).
- Goal: signup pillar (Pillar 2) — closes the display-name gap for magic-link users in the post flow. The contact-prefill cycle (`contact-prefill`) shipped name pre-fill for Google OAuth users; this cycle extends it to magic-link users without any new UI — just remembering what the user already typed. Friction removed: magic-link users type their name once, not every post. Rotating to Pillar 2 (last cycle was Pillar 1 — nnumber-autofill).

- What: **Homepage curated rails ("Browse curated collections") now show year+hours-controlled "Good deal" (emerald) or "Priced high" (amber) chips on aircraft cards** — the last aircraft surface without deal verdict chips. Previously, AircraftRailCards in HomeRails passed no `compVerdict`, so homepage rail cards showed no deal signal. Now HomeRails batch-fetches comps for every unique make+model family across all rail listings in one parallel `Promise.all` (one Supabase read per unique family, not per listing), computes `clubHangerDealVerdict` per listing self-excluding from its own comp set, and passes `compVerdict` to each `AircraftRailCard`. Previously, the rail used `clubHangerEstimate` (comparing against the entire make+model family, no year or hours control) — a 1970 Cessna 172 listed below the family median (which includes modern avionics-equipped 172s) would falsely show "Good deal." ## 2026-06-27T124751Z — PASS — alert-weekly-digest
- Pages: /api/cron/alert-digest (new internal cron endpoint; no user-facing page changes)
- What: **The email-alert subscriber loop is now complete.** Confirmed subscribers now receive a weekly digest when new matching listings exist. A new `GET /api/cron/alert-digest` route (called daily at 08:00 UTC by Vercel cron) queries all `status='confirmed'` alerts whose `last_digest_at` is older than 7 days (or null). For each, it parses the `source_path` (e.g. `/aircraft/cessna/172`, `/partnerships/near/khwd`, `/aircraft/for-sale/california`) into typed filter params, counts new aircraft or partnership listings since the subscriber's last digest, and if the count is > 0 sends a "N new {context} listings" email via `sendEmail` (no-ops safely if `RESEND_API_KEY` is absent) and stamps `last_digest_at`. Zero matches → alert skipped, `last_digest_at` unchanged (re-checked tomorrow). New `vercel.json` wires the daily cron schedule. New `buildAlertDigestEmail` in `src/lib/email.ts`. 3 files changed/created: `src/app/api/cron/alert-digest/route.ts` (new, 251 lines); `src/lib/email.ts` (add `buildAlertDigestEmail`); `vercel.json` (new cron config). **Human action needed:** set `CRON_SECRET` in the Vercel dashboard (Dashboard → Project → Settings → Environment Variables) and pass it in the Authorization header `Bearer $CRON_SECRET`; without it the route is unprotected (warning logged, not error). Also ensure `RESEND_API_KEY` and sender domain are verified in Resend before real emails go out.
- Goal: signup pillar (Pillar 2 adjacent) — completes the email-alert retention loop. Subscribers who opted in for Cessna 172 alerts (or any make/model/state/airport alerts) have been getting confirmation emails since slice 2 shipped, but NO follow-up when new listings appeared. This is the missing link: the digest that makes the subscription valuable. Previous two cycles were Pillar 1 (parts-wanted-filter) and Pillar 3 (deal-verdict-browse-chips). Pillar 2 P1 items are frozen on auth files; this is the highest-value doable Pillar 2 / retention item in the backlog ("Build slices 2-3 NOW").
